VOID_REVERSAL provenance without a one-to-one FK (2026-09-20 10:13Z; F-CTR record §6 item 2; 04 T-SL-01): a VOID_REVERSAL posting is one delta against the cumulative of possibly many postings, so `subledger_posting.reverses_posting_id` stays NULL for that entry kind and is not the traceability mechanism. The reversal's provenance is the approved void request: the posting's computation / command linkage names the request, whose subject content carries posted_line_count and posted_through (D-98 92); the reversed set is derived, not stored — every posting of the contract in the same book with recorded_at at or before posted_through — and the conservation identity Σ(reversed set) + reversal = 0 per account and book is asserted by a test on the CTL-046 world (fail-first). 04 T-SL-01 states the derivation and the identity in words (F-CTR, docs first, 04 rev 1.45). No link table pre-1.0; whether the auditor requires a stored reversal set (a posting-reversal link table) is a G12 question for Ray — recorded, not decided here. Data-model / traceability ruling, not an accounting change; posted amounts unchanged.
100. CTL-029 control facts derive from the actual rerun comparison (2026-09-20 10:18Z; Codex P4-SOP1-CTL029-R1 on 746ff16c; REQ-RPT-002, CTL-029; verified on main 6be2841a framework.py ~1007-1020 / _outcome ~858-884): the report job records CTL-029 PASS (population 1, exceptions 0) unconditionally and only afterwards computes output_sha256_equal / control_totals_equal for a rerun, returning SUCCEEDED either way — a persisted PASS can contradict a returned mismatch. Ruling: the rerun comparison is computed BEFORE the control fact is recorded and the fact is derived from it — first run (no rerun_of): PASS, detail first_run = true, the run's hash / totals as the reproducibility stamp; rerun of a SUCCEEDED original with both equal: PASS, exceptions 0; rerun with a hash-only or totals-only mismatch: FAIL, exceptions 1, detail carries rerun_of, both equality flags, the original's and the rerun's hash / totals (evidence retained, never overwritten); rerun of an original without output (original FAILED or hash / totals null): NOT_APPLICABLE, detail reason ORIGINAL_WITHOUT_OUTPUT. The run status and the job outcome stay SUCCEEDED when the rerun produced its output — the reproducibility failure is a control exception (the CTL-029 FAIL fact plus the false flags in the result), not a job failure — unless 05 §5.6 / REQ-RPT-002 text already says a mismatched rerun ends FAILED, in which case the documents govern and P4 reports the sentence before coding. Focused cases required: identical, hash-only mismatch, totals-only mismatch, first run, original without output — each checking the persisted control fact (result, counts, detail) and the returned flags. Owner: P4 (lane-p4-doctor; slice P4-CTL029-R1) on a fresh merge of the post-P5 head (P5 4a3120ec edits framework.py imports / release_identity and retargeted test_ctl_029_run_record_and_rerun; both corrections preserved). Control-evidence ruling, not an accounting change; no report values change.
99a. Addendum to 99 — the approved cutoff is durable (2026-09-20 10:31Z; Codex 1029 item 3, VOID-2 source review 0fe186ea): today posted_line_count / posted_through are transient hash members (the request stores digest + flags; audit / decision store the digest; impact preview / event / posting omit the cutoff), so the hash cannot recover the approved timestamp and "latest current state" is not an approved cutoff. The 99 implementation therefore stores the approved basis durably: the void request's stored subject content carries posted_line_count and posted_through as readable members (not only inside the digest), the approval decision record and the CONTRACT_VOIDED event / VOID_REVERSAL posting detail carry the request id and posted_through, and the derived reversed set reads that stored cutoff — never the latest state. Conservation asserted against the stored cutoff. Owner F-CTR (merge-prep slice). Traceability ruling; posted amounts unchanged.

Void approval: final content / route validation under the financial locks (2026-09-20 10:31Z; Codex F-CTR-VOID-FRESH-R3 on 0fe186ea / 073de590; TXN-04 READ COMMITTED 05:314): decide locks approval_request and checks the content hash, then void._approved compares the flags BEFORE taking the contract FOR UPDATE and the group lock; a deferred compute can lock the group, persist lines and commit after that read and before the first financial lock, so a one-step approval of an unposted contract can proceed over new postings. Ruling: the void path acquires the financial locks in the SAME order the posting path uses (group lock, then the contract row FOR UPDATE — the approval_request lock stays first since the posting path never takes it), and only THEN performs the final validation — recompute posted_line_count / posted_through and the route basis under the locks, refuse STALE_SUBJECT / stale-approval on any change — and holds the locks through execution in the same transaction; no pre-lock check is the last check. Required DB case: two sessions with a deterministic interleaving (the compute commits between the void path's pre-lock read and its first lock, forced through a monkeypatched module boundary in the test — no production-only seam) showing the stale refusal or the serialization; a sequential compute-before-approve test does not cover it. Owner F-CTR (slice VOID-2b, now; before merge prep). Control / concurrency ruling, not an accounting change.

Out-of-period register attribution — five ENG-C6 RPT-2 policy questions (2026-09-20 10:45Z; ENG-C6 report 8d28e252, code 3afa3fd1; D-98 95 / 96): (Q1) a trigger-only late delta (POLICY_RERUN, FX_REPUBLISH, RESTATE — no first-included event) is attributed to its TRIGGER, not refused: the register row's attribution kind is EVENT_SET or TRIGGER, a TRIGGER row names the computation id and the trigger code in place of the event set, conservation holds across both kinds, and OUT_OF_PERIOD_ATTRIBUTION_MISSING is reserved for a line with neither; (Q2) a line persisted before T-SL-12 exists refuses by name — and before 1.0 no backfill is needed (lane / test databases rebuild; the D-98 98 convention); from 1.0 a backfill is a deterministic IM-A append derived from contract_computation.cause_event_ids narrowed to the subject, ruled when the first such database exists; (Q3) as-of state reconstruction (REQ-PLT-030): a transition with a stored timestamp uses it; a transition without one (`failed` on the journal run) uses the SOP-7 audit event of that transition — never "now"; a transition with neither is unknown-as-of and the row refuses by name; (Q4) T-SL-08 journal-entry source_event_ids also take the union of the lines' T-SL-12 sets in ENG-06 order (summarise.py today reads contract_event_id only — the same gap one layer up): follow-up slice C6-RPT-3 after the l6 merge, docs first (S14 rule + 04 T-SL-08 note), no value change; (Q5) under DATASET_FREEZE the population is read as of the FREEZE instant (the close's own journal runs are part of the frozen dataset), not as of cutoff_known_at — consistent with D-98 96. Channel refund bound mirrors the engine's cover rule (2026-09-20 10:50Z; Codex F-CTR-BOUND-R1 / R2 on VOID-3a 66660cfd / 9dab127f; D-87 L6-5-Q-15 01-DECISIONS:474; 04 table 15.4-A; S10-R-07): the API / CSV v2 channel guard (`check_bounds` in domain/contracts/events.py) is a pre-check of the engine's REFUND_EXCEEDS_BILLED rule and must predict the engine, never a stricter or looser bound. (R1) Cover: a referenced credit consumes its own obligation's billing plus the REMAINING unreferenced contract billing; an unreferenced credit consumes the full non-VC stream; remaining cover is computed across prior credits and obligations in stream order (never each bucket independently — that would over-permit; never own-bucket-only — that under-permits, refusing the engine-covered unreferenced 300 → referenced 200 shape). (R2) Scope: under an effective LEGACY_PARITY preset the VC-stratified booking / added lines are excluded from the bound and from the non-VC stream exactly as stage-01 `_vc_lines` excludes them — the channel reads the effective preset it already resolves for the engine and classifies VC lines the same way; this is a conditional scope, not a blanket VC exception, and VC_CREDIT_EXCEEDS_ESTIMATE is untouched. The original VOID-3a values stay (EUR 100 + status 100 → credit 150 refused, credit 100 accepted). 04 §16.3 states that the channel bound is the engine cover rule (docs first, under F-CTR's 1.45 amendment). Implementation of 99a (2026-09-20 10:51Z; F-CTR VOID-2b e3383f4e): `approval_decision` carries no detail column, so the approved cutoff is stored on the request's preview (`before.posted_basis`), the CONTRACT_VOIDED payload (optional approval_request_id / posted_line_count / posted_through — the engine reads none), the execution audit event `contract.void_executed` (catalogued) and the VOID_REVERSAL posting description; the decision's own audit event carries it too. No shared-head migration for a decision detail column before 1.0; the audit trail is the durable carrier. Accepted as satisfying 99a.
101a. One lock order for the contract group (2026-09-20 10:51Z; F-CTR observation at VOID-2b): every path that takes both the contract-group lock and a contract row FOR UPDATE takes them in the posting path's order — group first, then contract — so the void path (VOID-2b), the compute path and the activation path cannot deadlock or interleave against each other; `activation.activate` / `submit_activation` today lock contract-then-group (the inverse) and are brought into the same order with a two-session interleaving test in the activation shape. Owner P5 (slice P5-LOCK-R1; sprint/l12-p5 on 065e7f65). Concurrency ruling, not an accounting change.
104. Joined keys are injective (2026-09-20 11:03Z; Codex C6-RPT-R5 on the out-of-period register event_key; the RPT41-ID-1 precedent; CV-21; D-98 85): no report, export, register or reader key is ever built by joining raw identifiers (external ids, codes, versions) with a delimiter. A composite identity is either a structural tuple of typed components (the in-memory / DB form) or, where a single string is required (row_key, dataset key, CSV cell), the CV-21 escaped canonical encoding of that tuple (each component percent-encoded — `%` first, then the delimiter — so the encoding is injective; an empty component keeps its position; a genuinely absent optional component encodes as the `%` marker where the schema needs it). Multi-member sets are the encoded members in ENG-06 order joined by the delimiter after encoding, so a member that itself contains the delimiter cannot merge two sets. Every such key names its encoding in the governed schema row (SCREENS_B / ENGINE_SPEC_B) and carries a collision test with a same-contract witness (e.g. external_id 'K:1|event:K' at stream_version 2 versus the two-event set of contract K at versions 1 and 2). Owners: ENG-C6 for out_of_period_register.event_key (in its merge prep); every lane that builds a joined key thereafter. Identity ruling, not an accounting change; monetary oracles unchanged.
99b. Deterministic reversed-set boundary (2026-09-20 11:06Z; Codex F-CTR-VOID-CUTOFF-R4 on VOID-2b e3383f4e; 04 ~3778): a timestamp cutoff (`recorded_at <= posted_through`) has no tie-order — under a frozen or coarse clock the VOID_REVERSAL posting R written at the same instant T as the last original posting is selected by `<= T`, so the derived set contains its own reversal, exceeds the stored population and the identity Σ(reversed) + R counts R twice (the ledger still nets to zero; this is a lineage / definition defect, not a money defect). Ruling: the stored cutoff is a strictly ordered position, not a timestamp — the void request's stored basis carries, per book, the seal-chain position of the last original posting at the read (`posted_through_seq` = the book's chain_seq / posting seal sequence, alongside posted_through and posted_line_count); the derived reversed set is every posting of the contract in that book with seal sequence <= the stored position (the reversal's own sequence is necessarily greater, so it is excluded by construction, never by entry-kind filtering alone); the derived count must equal the stored posted_line_count / posting count (mismatch → refusal by name CUTOFF_POPULATION_MISMATCH); conservation Σ(reversed) + R = 0 per account and book is asserted against that set. Both the advanced-clock test and the original no-advance CTL-046 population (same-timestamp) must pass; resolving by advancing the test clock alone is not accepted. No migration or link table (the basis is stored content). Owner F-CTR (slice VOID-3d, with VOID-3c before merge prep). Traceability ruling, not an accounting change.

Lock order covers implicit row locks and transitive callers (2026-09-20 11:25Z; Codex on P5-LOCK-R2 4fae8ff0): the 101a rule (group first, then contract) applies to every transaction that will touch BOTH the combination_group row and a contract row by any means — an explicit FOR UPDATE, an implicit UPDATE (events/stream.py `_raise_head` UPDATE contract :278 and `_mark_dirty` UPDATE combination_group :320), or a transitive call (append_events, compute_group) — not only to functions that call `lock_group` by name. Today `domain/contracts/events.py::record_events` (:824) takes the contract lock through `_locked_contract` (:475), then `append_events` (:830) updates the contract head and then the group (C → G), and `compute_group` (:841 / compute_job.py:520) takes the group lock in the same uow — a possible C → G / G → C wait cycle against the reordered `submit_activation` (repo.py :73 → :74). Ruling: the event entry paths (record_events and every other path that appends events or computes inside a uow) acquire the group lock before the first contract row lock, through the shared helper (unlocked read for the group id → lock_group → contract FOR UPDATE → membership re-verified); the inventory is by lock effect (explicit locks, UPDATE statements, transitive callers), recorded in the lane record; a deterministic two-session interleaving case for record_events vs submit_activation (A holds C, B holds G) proves the absence of the cycle; membership revalidation, If-Match and existing authorization preserved. Owner P5 (slice P5-LOCK-R3, before its merge). Concurrency ruling, not an accounting change.
102a. Addendum to 102 — proven trigger-only and as-of history across retries (2026-09-20 11:31Z; Codex C6 follow-on source review at 976cd8d2 / ce8e0f47, persisting at 2b9b7a7b): (Q1 / R3) a register row is a TRIGGER row only when PERSISTED subject-specific evidence proves the computation had no first-included event for the subject — the computation's stored cause / lineage for that subject is empty AND its trigger kind is a non-event trigger (POLICY_RERUN, FX_REPUBLISH, RESTATE) recorded on the computation itself; an empty T-SL-12 set with no such proof (including every historical NULL-event cumulative line persisted before T-SL-12) is MISSING lineage and refuses by name OUT_OF_PERIOD_ATTRIBUTION_MISSING (ENGINE_SPEC_B pre-T-SL-12 refusal; no backfill pre-1.0). (Q3 / R4) the as-of state of a journal run is reconstructed from the run's FULL transition history — every SOP-7 audit event of the run (approved, failed, retried, succeeded) with its occurrence time — through the actual reader, independent of the run's CURRENT state: the state as of the cutoff is the latest transition at or before the cutoff across all retry cycles; a later failure or a later successful retry never hides an earlier eligible transition; composed-path cases required — approval day 1, failure day 3, re-export day 3 + 6 h with cutoff day 3 + 1 h → failed; a later successful retry → the state at the cutoff, not the current one; repeated failures → the eligible one. Owner ENG-C6 (slice C6-RPT-4 before its merge). Reporting rulings, not accounting changes.
102b. Group-scoped lineage for member lines (2026-09-20 11:44Z; ENG-C6 Q-C6-RPT-6 at C6-RPT-4 6a4fc379): a combination group is one allocation unit, so a COMMAND computation whose first-included event belongs to member B legitimately moves member A's allocation; A's out-of-period lines then have an empty SUBJECT lineage but a real event cause. Ruling: S14-R-13a lineage is the subject's own first-included events when there are any; when the subject's own set is empty the lineage is the GROUP's first-included events of that computation (all members, ENG-06 order), attributed as EVENT_SET with a `lineage_scope` attribute of SUBJECT or GROUP on the register row (docs: S14-R-13a, S15-R-18b, SCREENS_B RPT-16 column); the TRIGGER proof of 102a applies only when the group's set is also empty; OUT_OF_PERIOD_ATTRIBUTION_MISSING stays for lines with neither lineage nor proof (incl. every pre-T-SL-12 line). Conservation unchanged. Owner ENG-C6 (slice C6-RPT-5 before its merge). Reporting-attribution ruling, not an accounting change.
101c. Combination order, recovery contract and approval revalidation (2026-09-20 12:03Z; Codex sealed R3b review c28ab8e4… on d2b1cdc3): (a) NO automatic retry exists on the approval path (api/v1/approvals → deps → approvals/engine → subjects → apply_combination: the unit of work rolls back and rethrows; problems.py maps no 40P01 and returns 500; _settle_failed_command abandons the attempt; the frontend sends once) — the record's retry claim is corrected; the ordering / recovery contract is resolved WITHOUT assuming a retry: every path that touches combination_group rows takes ALL of them in one globally ascending id order — the target included, so the target is not taken before the sorted rest — then the proposal / judgement record, then the contract rows ascending; the judgement path (`judgements._recompute_contract`) adopts the same order (group before its own record) so no pair is inverted anywhere; a residual 40P01, if PostgreSQL ever reports one, maps to a NAMED retryable refusal (409 / 503 class with a problem code, not 500) and the client may resubmit — no automatic retry is designed or claimed. (b) Membership revalidation proves EQUALITY: each member's current group id equals the group id observed at the unlocked read (not merely "in the locked set"); any difference → REGROUPED. (c) The approval subject content of a group / proposal approval (COMBINATION_JOIN / LEAVE, the judgement-record approvals) includes the member rows' current group id and stream head at request time, so a member moved or appended between request and decision makes the decision STALE_SUBJECT (the D-98 92 pattern) — a qualified revalidation gap today, not an observed stale approval. (d) Deterministic wait evidence in the DB tests is bound to the PARTICIPANTS: the waiting backend is the request's own session (its pg_backend_pid captured by the request thread) and the blocker is the holder (pg_blocking_pids), not "any waiter whose SQL mentions combination_group". Owner P5 (slice P5-LOCK-R3c, before re-entering the merge queue). Concurrency / control ruling, not an accounting change.
106. Array reference columns in the snapshot registry (2026-09-20 12:03Z; Codex F-SNP-PREP-S2 on a9fb60fe): a column declared as an ordered array of references (migration_batch.import_upload_ids, REPLAY, UUID[]) declares its element target table and class like a scalar reference; export applies the scalar rule PER ELEMENT preserving order — an element whose target is in the copied set is kept, an element whose target is REGENERATED or cut is dropped with the drop recorded in the export manifest (count per column), and an element whose target is absent from the source is a refusal by name (broken target) — the reference scanner covers array columns and the inventory drift guard refuses an undeclared array reference; the tenant / cutoff rules of the element target apply. Today the column is always [] (no non-empty domain writer), so this is declared coverage, not a current export bug; policy defined before any non-empty coverage is accepted. Owner F-SNP (docs first in the registry rules; tests kept / cut / absent). Snapshot-data ruling, not an accounting change.
102c. Addendum to 102 / 102b — transition ordering and mixed-scope rows (2026-09-20 12:12Z; Codex RPT4-H1 + RPT5 mixed-scope finding on eecb754d / d4f2c081): (H1) the as-of reconstruction orders a run's transitions by (occurred_at, audit chain_seq) — the audit chain's durable sequence breaks equal-time ties, never the state's spelling; the reader reads every audit record shape that changes a run's state (journal_run after.state AND the batch-level roll-up record journal_batch after.run_state), and an equal-time / later-mutation control (declared failure then retry / export at the same instant, acknowledgement later; cutoff between) must select the later transition; helper-supplied Transition tests are not end-to-end audit-history acceptance — the retry writer / reader integration remains to be proved on a DB. (Mixed scope) an aggregated register row never mixes lineage scopes: the scope is part of the row IDENTITY — the string row_key / event_key gains an encoded scope component (…:SUBJECT / …:GROUP, CV-21 encoded) so the same event set attributed as SUBJECT for one member and GROUP for another yields two rows with distinct stable identities; both input orders give identical output; line count and monetary conservation hold across the split; typed event identity / encoding unchanged. Owner ENG-C6 (slice C6-RPT-6 before its merge). Reporting-attribution rulings, not accounting changes.

Scope arrays are never emptied by element cuts (2026-09-20 12:42Z; Codex FSNP-ARRAY-SCOPE-1 / 2 on b94828d0; 04 T-REF-13; 0007_roles CHECK): an array reference whose EMPTY value carries a meaning of its own — gl_account.entity_ids ([] = all entities, T-REF-13) and role_assignment.entity_ids ([] must equal is_all_entities per the load CHECK) — is declared a SCOPE array (`empty_means_all` / `paired_flag`) in the snapshot registry, and the per-element rule of 106 is qualified for it: a partial cut keeps the remaining non-empty scope (the excluded targets do not exist in the copy, so the effective scope over the surviving entities is unchanged — counted in array_drops as before); a COMPLETE cut of a non-empty scope array is a refusal by name (SNAPSHOT_SCOPE_UNREPRESENTABLE, naming table, column, row id and the cut targets) — the copy never turns a restriction into universal applicability, and the paired boolean is never flipped to represent it; rows whose array is empty at the source keep [] with their flag; unchanged rows unchanged. Snapshot-data ruling, not an accounting change.
101d. addendum to 101c (recorded 2026-09-20 06:07 PDT, after 107; Codex 1304 items 1–2; supervisor ruling, not Ray's) 1. **Hold before hash.** The approved basis (subject content hash and head) of an approval request is captured only after every system-side mutation of the subject performed in the same unit of work — in particular submit_judgement's mandatory system hold (HOLD_APPLIED advances the head) precedes the hash/store, so an ACTIVE judgement with a PENDING human route and no external edit approves unchanged instead of voiding STALE_SUBJECT (P5-LOCK-R3C-SELF-STALE-R1). 2. **Basis re-validated under the protecting locks.** Before an approved command is applied (apply_combination, review_judgement, and any on_approved path), the approved head/hash is compared again while the group → record → contract locks are held, in the same globally ascending order; a mismatch is the same named STALE_SUBJECT refusal, never a retry (P5-LOCK-R3C-WAIT-FRESH-R2). Membership/proposal equality remains a separate check; it does not substitute for the basis check. 3. **Stored 409.** The mapped 409 lock-conflict (40P01 → `lock-conflict`, ERR-52) is a STORED terminal command status (api/deps.py); only the named unstored statuses / 5xx abandon the attempt. Client behaviour after it is an explicit resubmission with the idempotency semantics the dev-guide states; there is no automatic retry (DG-KRN-DB-08 correction; dev-guide 1.40 assigned to P5). Dependents blocked: P5 merge (hold); F-CTR's shared-helper switch waits for the corrected P5 head. Not blocked: F-CLO, C1b, P2, F-SNP, ENG-C6, ENG-C8, P1, T1F, F-ADM.

Migrations never read live Python enums (recorded 2026-09-20 06:25 PDT; wave-3 chain STOP at fadm, main d350a37a; supervisor ruling, engineering — not an accounting judgement) Fact: on a fresh test database (schema drop + `alembic upgrade head`, tests/conftest.py test_database) migration 0004 creates `security_event_kind` from the live `SecurityEventKind` Python enum, so the type already carries INVITATION_LOOKUP_FAILED and 0058's `add_enum_value` fails with DuplicateObject ("enum label already exists"); on an existing 0057 database 0058 applies. 0058 is the first add_enum_value revision, so the history was never exercised this way. Ten DB-fixture unit tests error; post-check cpu rc1. Ruling: (1) a migration never imports or reads a live Python enum; every revision that creates an enum type pins its labels as a literal tuple equal to the labels at that revision's authoring state (for every enum created so far the literal equals the current members, except security_event_kind which excludes INVITATION_LOOKUP_FAILED); (2) a label enters the history only through an add_enum_value revision (DG-MIG-06 unchanged); (3) a CPU mirror test asserts literal-at-creation ∪ later add_enum_value labels == the Python enum's members for every enum type, and an architecture test forbids erev_api.enums imports under db/migrations/versions. Owner F-ADM (F-ADM-MIG-R1), fix-forward on main; dev-guide 1.41. Database proof = the fix merge's post-check (the ten fixture tests on erev_test). Dependents blocked: P1 (p1r2), F-CLO and every later lane whose post-check runs the fixture tests; the Ray-side dev DB 0057 → 0058 migration waits [CORRECTION 2026-09-20 13:26 PDT, supervisor read-only observation: the dev DB `erev` reads Alembic 0054 (version table last written 2026-09-17 15:38); "0057" was an unmeasured premise carried since COMMIT 29 — the item now reads 0054 → 0061, RELEASED at the p2 landing fa87c357; historical text kept as written] for the fix to land (0058 itself is unchanged).
101d-note. every on_approved hook; subject content excludes decision-derived state (recorded 2026-09-20 06:27 PDT; P5 R3d scope question) 101d(2) applies to every on_approved hook (apply_combination, review_judgement, activation.activate, estimates._approve_version, _apply_event_submission, _apply_ssp_override, and any later hook): the approved basis is re-validated under the hook's own protecting locks before its first write. A subject content function describes the subject only — fields the decision itself changes before the hook (approval status, decided_at, approver, request linkage) are excluded from the content, so the pre-lock hash and the under-lock hash compare the same thing; skipping the check for a hook, or comparing against a value re-hashed after the decision's own writes, is not an admissible reading. Owner P5 (P5-LOCK-R3d-b), before the merge hold lifts; dev-guide 1.40 row extended in place.
107a. extension of 107: personas follow the provisioned grants (recorded 2026-09-20 06:55 PDT; Codex 1354 F-RPS-ACT2-PERSONA-R1; supervisor ruling, engineering / test composition — not an accounting judgement) Answer-key personas map to the platform roles that actually carry each operation's grant in the provisioned permission catalogue. The plan's operation → persona composition is test-side (never an original input or expected value) and follows the governed operation-owner statements. Author / approver separation is preserved per operation family: the persona that creates / submits an SSP book version never approves it (ak-ssp-analyst = ssp_analyst; ak-ssp-approver = ssp_approver, or the catalogue's equivalent); journal runs are executed by a persona holding journal.run (revenue_accountant / ak-preparer); report.run remains controller's positive control. Personas never receive fabricated permissions and no check is weakened. A governed operation-owner statement that names a role the catalogue does not grant is a specification inconsistency reported to the PRD / G12 owner, never fixed by granting. Owner F-RPS; dev-guide 1.42; PLAT-G4-1 treats the five original plans as executable only after this composition holds.

"CUTOFF-R1 basis" (11:45 PDT; SUPERVISOR RULING — engineering; no accounting figure touched; Codex 1826 finding + 1838 preflight; F-RPS design): two cutoff bases for contract-version selection in reports — RECORD basis (L6-3-Q-19: max(known_at, transaction time); right when known_at defaulted to the application clock because computation.persist stamps versions with the events' server recorded_at; dashboards and default runs) and HISTORICAL basis (READ-1: an explicit as-of read keeps the caller-supplied known_at exactly). The basis is a STORED additive report-run parameter `known_at_basis` ∈ {record, historical}, default record, set to historical by framework._resolve when the caller supplied known_at (computed over the caller-supplied parameters before normalisation; never inferred from the timestamp value or normalised presence), honoured after validation when caller-supplied (`historical` without known_at → 422; `record` with known_at → explicit compatibility read), stored per D-87 as a schema key, shown in RV-03, and read back by report_params(run) so run, rerun and explain resolve identically. Docs-first: 04 1.54 (§16.9 API-S-ReportRunCreate), SCREENS_B 1.18 (RPT-R-01, RV-03), dev-guide 1.39 (rule sentence). With basis = record every consumer is byte-for-byte today's behaviour. POS-117 fixture, four values, March period, after_seq 7 unchanged; cutoff not advanced. Never attributed to Ray.

"Q-3 line record time" (12:19 PDT; SUPERVISOR RULING — engineering; no accounting figure touched; Codex 1849 finding; F-RPS return): subledger lines carry recorded_at = the application clock (uow.now; sealed LINE_MEMBERS member; DG-KRN-TIME-01) while contract versions, contract events and checkpoint stamps carry server time — so under the HISTORICAL cutoff basis a line whose posting committed after the checkpoint stamp but with an earlier application clock in the same period is admitted (production window = request duration; runner window unbounded). Ruling = option (b): add a NEW server-stamped column on T-SL-04 (writer-set from one `SELECT transaction_timestamp()` per transaction, or a DB default — the implementing lane states which), used ONLY by the historical-basis line predicate (API-C-10 historical variant); recorded_at keeps its application-clock meaning and DG-KRN-TIME-01 is unchanged; the LINE_MEMBERS seal is unchanged (the new column is not a member); existing rows nullable, the historical predicate falling back to recorded_at for NULL (legacy rows documented, never inferred as server time); other lanes' recorded_at pins untouched; docs-first 04 T-SL-04 + API-C-10 (+ ENGINE_SPEC_B where it cites the predicate); DG-MIG-12; the NEW Alembic revision is numbered after the last landed revision at its landing time — a separate slice (frps4) dispatched after ENG-C6's 0064 lands (0065 on 0064). Until then the window is documented (option (c)) and the DB-bound exclusion evidence stays xfail(strict=True) naming this candidate — a fail-first marker, not acceptance. Never attributed to Ray. - 113 REFINEMENT (12:22 PDT; Codex 1922; adopted into the ruling): a NULL → recorded_at fallback for legacy T-SL-04 rows does NOT close historical correctness (at fe17794d line recorded_at, posting / line / seal created_at and sealed_at all use uow.now; related event / version time can predate a deferred posting; no persisted server timestamp exists to substitute). Legacy rows need a PROVABLE RECONSTRUCTION of their record time or an EXPLICIT NAMED REFUSAL wherever their unknown record time could affect the requested historical result; documenting the fallback alone is not acceptable. The new column's clock semantics must be specified precisely — transaction_timestamp() / now() = transaction-START time, clock_timestamp() = call time, neither a commit-order guarantee — and the long-transaction / checkpoint boundary tested before any claim of arbitrary later-commit exclusion; business-time mapping and seals preserved. Open through frps4 and final G4 / release acceptance; frps3's strict XFAIL is unresolved evidence, not a fix. - 113 IMPLEMENTATION REQUIREMENTS (12:28 PDT; Codex 1927 — sealed design note PRODUCTION-F-RPS-Q3-LINE-TIME-DESIGN-fe17794d.md; adopted into the ruling for frps4): both aging layer amounts and revenue-date selection use the stated historical basis; effective_date / recorded_at and the mapping inputs preserved; the new table column must not silently extend the dynamic LINE_MEMBERS seal payload (seal-excluded by construction, asserted); database-authored provenance and immutability for the new seal-excluded metadata with application-role controls (no application-role UPDATE of the record-time column); legacy rows → explicit named refusal or proven reconstruction (1922); transaction-start / write time distinguished from historical committed-population evidence — the long-transaction / checkpoint boundary tested before any later-commit-exclusion claim; POS-117's four cells and seq-7 cutoff unchanged. - 113 slice naming (12:38 PDT): the Q-3 record-time-column implementation is chain slice frps5 (after engc6's 0064 lands; 0065 on 0064); frps4 is now the F-RPS snapshot-dataset registry (RPS-SNAP, Codex 1936 item 3). 115. "gate-slot staleness" (supervisor engineering ruling, 2026-09-20 15:28 PDT; not an accounting decision) Fact: the two-slot gate protocol (`~/dev/erev-wt/logs/gate-slot-N.d`, claimed by atomic `mkdir`, an `owner` file with pid + timestamp, released by the owner) had no written rule for a claim whose owner appears stale. At 15:25:00 lane T1's gate chain removed lane F-CTR's LIVE owner file (claimed 15:24:15, pytest running) as "stale" and wrote its own, so three CPU gate runs ran concurrently. Ruling: (1) a slot claim is released only by its owner (the pid in `owner`); (2) a claim may be called stale only when `ps -p <owner pid>` shows the pid gone; (3) even then the lane REPORTS the stale claim to the supervisor (name, owner line, time) and does not remove it — the supervisor removes it after confirming; (4) a lane never writes into a slot directory it did not create; (5) a run that started under a slot obtained by removing another lane's claim is not a slot-governed run and is re-run. Docs: NONE — the two-slot gate protocol is a supervisor lane-practice rule (it appears in no governed document; the dev-guide has no DG-TST slot text), so no governed-spec edit and no lane owner; the rule lives in this candidate, the supervisor record and the lanes' own records (which name the incident). A governed DG-TST row may be proposed later by a lane through docs-first; not now.

The same checks pass on the pre-merge tree. Ruling: (1) the landed governed rule supersedes the tests' pre-1.24 expectation — the tests adopt the shared checker's public names (`support.trace_linkage`: measure == column, or the boundary form, or an alias; `balance_link_columns`), keeping every value / unit / lineage tie, the pinned UNLINKED / STALE sets and the CV-56 measures; (2) the scaling property classifies the quantity nodes (`delivered_quantity`, `delivered_quantity_cum`, `returned_quantity_cum`, `quantity`) as DIMENSIONLESS (equality asserted under 10^k money scaling) and the two unit-revenue rates as EXACT_AMOUNT_COLUMNS; the fail-closed guard for unclassified measures stays; (3) no numeric tolerance, oracle or expected value changes; (4) the record names the superseding rule and quotes the three pre-fix failures as measured; (5) READY only after a green re-run of all four gates under a genuinely free slot. ### 116 ADDENDUM — encoding bound on exact-amount columns (2026-09-20 16:15 PDT; supervisor engineering ruling) Fact: stored exact-amount columns carry the governed 18-place encoding of their node's value (CV-51 / DG-KRN-EXP-03); on the pinned two-contract example `allocated_exact` differs from exact × 10 by three units of 10⁻¹⁸, so the scaling property's exact-equality assertion for stored exact columns fails for an encoding reason, not a calculation one. Ruling: the property may bound STORED exact-amount columns by exactly the encoding tolerance the governed rule defines and that the property already applies to nodes — no looser or ad hoc epsilon; only for columns carrying that encoding; exact equality stays asserted wherever no encoding applies; posted bounds, the fail-closed guard for unclassified measures and every financial oracle unchanged; the record cites the rule and quotes the measured difference. Not a tolerance loosening of any answer key. #### 116 ADDENDUM — provenance criteria (2026-09-20 16:44 PDT; supervisor engineering ruling incorporating Codex 2329 / 2341) The encoding allowance applies ONLY where the committed engine's actual encoded producer branch produced the stored value: (a) `allocated_exact` — one encoded residue from its allocated node; its no-node segment-sum fallback is raw rational → exact equality there; (b) `original_ssp_selected` — an encoded residual node only for a residual candidate WITH that node present; ordinary SSP, the missing-residual-node fallback and the zero paths are raw → exact; (c) contract `total_ssp` — one node encoding sum(raw weights), with a raw resolved-SSP-sum fallback → exact on the fallback; distinct from the obligation `original_total_contract_ssp`, which stays raw and exact. Raw rational SSP / allocation columns keep exact equality; provenance / lineage checks on branch mismatches stay. The bound (1+|F|)/(2·10¹⁸) — verified correct for two 18-place half-up encodings — covers the FINAL encoding only when the underlying values satisfy X' = F·X; earlier posted-basis, targeted or realised monetary rounding is a separate derivation and is never hidden inside it. No member-count multiplier for total_ssp (its writer sums raw weights before encoding once). Unit revenue rates are quotients of TraceBuilder.exact numerator / denominator stored separately from the encoded node — they keep EXACT equality (no allowance ruled; any future allowance must follow that derivation, since division magnifies input encoding error). No universal tolerance for every erev.exact amount field; posted bounds, quantity / ratio invariance, lineage, failure sets and original oracles unchanged. Sources: PRODUCTION-T1-EXACT-ENCODING-ADVISORY-71d81d51.md (SHA 4e923495…), PRODUCTION-T1-THREE-COLUMN-PROVENANCE-71d81d51.md (SHA b92257df…). ### 116 ADDENDUM 3 — derivation-based encoding bound for the unit revenue rate columns (supervisor ruling, 2026-09-20 17:38 PDT; engineering / test composition; never attributed to Ray) **Trigger (measured).** Lane T1's wave-3 gate chain on `e57dbf85` (merge of main `f1f36bf5` into `sprint/l9`): `STAGE properties-ci rc=1` — `test_prop_metamorphic.py::test_scaling_by_powers_of_ten`, both pinned explicit examples (BHD_DEBIT_SPLIT_EXAMPLE k=1, BHD_TWO_CONTRACT_POOL_EXAMPLE), column `remaining_unit_revenue_rate` on K-1/POB-01: `assert Fraction(967741935483871, 200000000000000000) == 10 * Fraction(96774193548387, 200000000000000000)` — |y − 10x| = 5·10⁻¹⁸. The numerator carries the single 18-place encoding of the amount node; divided by q it becomes the rate's error. **Ruling.** (1) The engine is correct and unchanged: the quotient is exact per CV-47 (b) as written; no engine, answer-key, golden or oracle change. (3) Their bound follows the derivation, as Codex 2341 required ("must follow that derivation (division can magnify input encoding error) rather than reuse the flat node tolerance"): |y − F·x| ≤ (1 + F) / (2·10¹⁸) ÷ q, with q = the base row's scale-invariant quantity (`original_quantity` / `remaining_quantity`, asserted equal on both sides by the dimensionless law and > 0; a zero quantity stores 0 and is compared exactly). (4) The three earlier encoded columns (`allocated_exact`, residual-candidate `original_ssp_selected`, contract `total_ssp`) keep the flat single-encoding bound of the ADDENDUM (encoding bound); every raw column keeps exact equality; posted bounds, quantity / ratio invariance, lineage checks, the fail-closed guard, all oracles, test names and decorators unchanged. (5) This supersedes the "unit revenue rates exact" clause of the ADDENDUM (provenance criteria) for exactly these two columns WHEN PRODUCED; it is a test-composition ruling, never a tolerance on posted or exact amounts. **Runtime coverage (T1's read-only probe, 102 worlds / 224 obligation rows, producer-attributed; to be quoted in the record and READY):** `allocated_exact` encoded branch 224/224 (no-node fallback never exercised — inspection-only); `original_ssp_selected` raw 224/224 (residual-candidate encoded branch never exercised — inspection-only); contract `total_ssp` encoded 102/102 (raw fallback never exercised); unit revenue rates non-null 224/224 on `e57dbf85` (runtime-exercised now). Codex 0020's closure text and limit ("source closure only …") are quoted verbatim in the record. **Ordinary-touch item (Codex 0031):** the inherited "NULL today" rate comment is stale (T1F now produces rates) — fix in the same commit, without any additional tolerance or runtime pass/fail claim. **Docs:** NONE (test-only; the dev-guide §9.7 Metamorphic row already describes the law; if T1 finds the row states exact equality for the rates, amend that row docs-first in the same slice and say so). **Status:** GO given to T1 for fix-forward 2 as proposed — lint-guarded commit, the four gates under a genuinely free pid-first slot, record, READY. Codex retest requested on the committed head. ### 116 ADDENDUM 3 — REVISION 1 (supervisor, 2026-09-20 17:44 PDT; per Codex production-20260921-0041; supersedes the tolerance form of ADDENDUM 3 BEFORE any commit used it) **Corrections to ADDENDUM 3 as first written.** (a) Arithmetic: the observed difference is 1/(2·10¹⁷) = **5·10⁻¹⁸ = five quanta of 10⁻¹⁸**, not one. (b) Representation (committed e57dbf85 source, cited by Codex): `output_measures.py` 178–187 reads `TraceBuilder.exact(top) / TraceBuilder.exact(bottom)` and 204 saves that RAW quotient; the assembler (749) publishes the saved quotient; `tb.node` 188–203 separately encodes the rate TRACE as Q18(Nhat/qhat) without quantising the producer's returned quotient; for the remaining numerator, `schedule.py` 429–458 / 572–583 supplies posted P and raw N when unadjusted, and TraceBuilder stores P + Q18(N − P) on reconstruction; the denominator node supplies qhat = Q18(q). So the COLUMN is Nhat / qhat (not a final rounded rate) and the exact identity is Δrate = (Nhat′ − F·Nhat) / qhat. (c) A rate tolerance of (1+F)/(2·10¹⁸) ÷ q is NOT granted — it is only the special case in which the raw numerator scales exactly; posted / upstream numerator differences must remain explicit, the original rate (S05-R-16 x_p ÷ Q) receives no allowance by analogy, and Codex's analysis "is NOT approval of a replacement tolerance". **Revised ruling (engineering / test composition; engine, keys, oracles unchanged).** (1) For every obligation row whose rate node is linked, the scaling law asserts the EXACT QUOTIENT IDENTITY on both the base and the scaled world: `remaining_unit_revenue_rate == exact(remaining_allocation node) ÷ exact(remaining_quantity node)` and `original_unit_revenue_rate == exact(original_allocated_exact node) ÷ exact(original_quantity node)`, using the linked NUMERATOR and DENOMINATOR nodes' exact values (value + rounding_residue) — never the separately encoded rate node. (2) It asserts the decoded denominator is invariant under scaling (qhat′ == qhat; the quantity is dimensionless). (3) The rate's scaling behaviour is thereby reduced to the numerator node's OWN law — the remaining-allocation node under the posted bounds and the flat single-encoding bound already ruled (ADDENDUM — encoding bound); the original_allocated_exact node under the same flat bound — so NO new tolerance exists anywhere; the "unit revenue rates exact" clause of the provenance addendum is honoured as exact identity. (4) Zero quantity: the stored 0 is compared exactly; NULL rate: no node → None on both sides. (5) Adjusted / posted-only, changed-denominator and missing-input cases: the identity check applies unchanged (it is an identity, not a bound); a row where the identity fails is a defect of the producer or the trace, returned with the row, never tolerated. **Prerequisites before the code change.** (i) T1 publishes the sanitized capsule of the ORIGINAL 17:16:01–17:22:39 e57dbf85 failure from captured data only (candidate / tree; command, profile, seed / Hypothesis reproduction data; base / scaled WorldSpec and factor; failing row / column and rate link; numerator / denominator node ids, measures, values, rounding_residue; decoded operands and raw N / q where captured incl. the adjusted / unadjusted branch; exact column values and the encoded rate-node values; capture source named; later working-tree experiments distinguished; uncaptured operands marked unavailable). (ii) T1 confirms the identity check is feasible in the property worlds (node exact values reachable from the bundle) with the source lines for both rates. (iii) Supervisor confirmation → lint-guarded commit → gates under a free slot → record → READY → Codex retest. **Status:** T1 HELD (fix-forward 2 not to be committed; if committed, superseded — never amended). Independent rational calculation, not a native booking-world reproduction or an observed posted-money discrepancy. **Correction (ENG-T1F, lane-eng-b3, sprint/l19):** compute the ORIGINAL producer from the raw original quota and the raw original quantity; preserve NULL-at-zero, routed-out behaviour and genuine source links; preserve REPLAY — the raw operands must remain available through bound producing sources or an appropriate formula representation (a precomputed answer parameter alone does not establish provenance; changing the column while requiring it to equal the quotient of decoded trace inputs would create another inconsistency). No spec weakening, no rate tolerance. **Answer keys / goldens:** never edited by the lane — any key whose expected value pins the decoded-quotient figure is REPORTED with the id and the two values for a recorded ruling. **Docs:** ENGINE_SPEC docs-first only if a clarifying sentence is needed (the contract already says raw); record in the ENG-T1F lane record. Respect the active main-batch freeze (lane work only; no main touch; CPU gates under slot 2). **Status:** DISPATCHED to ENG-T1F 2026-09-20 18:14 PDT; Codex retest of the committed head requested via the lane's READY.

Lanes: F-SNP READY after CPU + 220 under a slot; the next integrated batch measures the DB-bound cases. ### 116 ADDENDUM 3 — REVISION 4 (supervisor, 2026-09-20 18:42 PDT; per Codex production-20260921-0139) — the NARROWED REMAINING-ONLY law is PERMITTED to be implemented; requirements binding **Codex's current-generator review is complete** (PRODUCTION-T1-GENERATOR-REMAINING-LAW-e57dbf85.md SHA 844055c1…): "A remaining-only change is supported for the positively established current input family, subject to the following implementation requirements (scratch patch4 itself is NOT reviewed or approved)". The four requirements, adopted verbatim as the ruling: (1) POSITIVE WHITELIST of the constructed bundle / input family — ordinary distinct STANDARD PRINCIPAL obligations; only POINT_IN_TIME or TIME_ELAPSED DAILY / MONTHLY_EVEN; positive observable POINT / AMOUNT SSP and prices; inception booking / activation plus DELIVERY / BILLING only; unchanged dates / calendars / contracts / quantities / order; no returns or return-policy pins, modifications, special methods, payment / deposit release, VC / royalty / extra components, holds / manual / posted input, residual / bundle SSP, specialist policies / features — inspected on the CONSTRUCTED inputs / defaults as well as WorldSpec so future constructor changes cannot silently widen eligibility; output zero returns and a method blacklist are insufficient. (2) Keep strict raw original_allocated_exact scaling, raw progress_ratio invariance (0 ≤ f ≤ 1), raw AND decoded remaining-quantity invariance, exact node subjects / expected source links and branch correspondence; on active unchanged inception FIXED, current raw X is tied to original raw X and N = X·(1 − f); the pre-recognition guard has f = 0, E = 0, N = X — not an inactive segment; a genuine inactive / no-extra branch requires N = 0 / current X = 0 and its own proof, never substituting original X; current complete generator builds have inception-active segments at version date ≥ inception. (3) Per-world independent raw-numerator ANCHOR |N̂ − X_current·(1 − f)| ≤ 1/(2·10¹⁸) on the eligible active branch (or the proved-zero inactive branch), AND the cross-world numerator bound |N̂′ − F·N̂| ≤ (1+F)/(2·10¹⁸); then, for equal nonzero q, the exact per-world column identity rate = N̂ / q and the derived bound |rate′ − F·rate| ≤ (1+F)/(2·10¹⁸·|q|); no currency / minor-unit allowance, no inherited node drift, no observed-delta fit, no extra final-column rounding term; q = 0 stays exact zero; missing nodes / NULL preserve and explain the corresponding eligibility rather than pass as zero. (4) An input outside the theorem fails a NAMED prerequisite or uses a separately justified strict law — never inherits this tolerance; `_assert_scaled_nodes` and all other financial assertions / oracles unchanged; the ORIGINAL rate remains STRICT, dependent on ENG-T1F 117's source closure and landing; the overwritten original 1-failed / 23-passed record preserved and later probes distinguished. **Status:** "permission to implement the narrowed law with assigned docs revision and committed source review, NOT acceptance of scratch code or runtime"; the landing order through ENG-T1F 117 still applies. **Docs revision ASSIGNED: dev-guide 1.49** (F-RPS 1.47 on main; F-SNP 1.48 on its lane) for T1's docs-first successor row (the superseded 56868377 row is removed by that commit). "an approval hook consumes the basis of ITS OWN request; the import basis is consumed once under the locks before the importer's effects" (supervisor engineering ruling, 2026-09-20 19:36 PDT; 101d refinement; per Codex production-20260921-0216 / 0226; never attributed to Ray; not an accounting decision) **Trigger (measured):** the integrated batch on main 0cb36c14 — every `legacy_contract_setup` import COMMIT failed with `import.commit_failed error_class=StaleBasis` (ci ~55 failures; parity 121 / 122 "golden step 02 did not commit"). **Cause (Codex-confirmed at source):** the import approval content includes every named contract's current head (approvals/subjects.py 865–934; absent = [key, None]); the legacy setup application books / replaces contracts (and may append COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED) before `_activate`, which passes the ORIGINAL IMPORT_COMMIT approval id into `activation.activate`; activation 794 calls the generic `assert_fresh_basis` (101d), which rehashes the IMPORT subject — the import's own authorised writes moved the heads → self-authored effects rejected. **Ruling (binding; Codex 0216 wording adopted):** "Correct the SUBJECT-SPECIFIC CONSUMPTION boundary under the authoritative locks BEFORE importer effects, and carry an explicit validated composition relationship into activation. Preserve direct CONTRACT_ACTIVATION freshness under group/contract locks and original approval/event lineage. Do not globally disable freshness, erase request IDs, rewrite an approved hash after effects, or permit arbitrary request types." Per Codex 0226 the existing controls are credited (job `_check_bases` rejects changes visible at its initial check; upload locking; immutable inputs; the downstream locked expected-head check) — no duplicate whole-import hashing; the remaining interval is `_check_bases`' plain SELECT without the affected locks and a later plan's reread adopting a current head as its expected head → retain the approved bases / expected absence into PROTECTED consumption (lock-bound), atomic rollback on a stale later plan. Design (P5): the IMPORT basis is validated once, under the authoritative locks (DG-KRN-DB-08 order), at the job's consumption boundary before any effect, refusing atomically; it yields an explicit validated COMPOSITION RELATIONSHIP carried through the apply context into legacy activation; `activate` admits either its own CONTRACT_ACTIVATION request (direct check, unchanged) or that verified composition (same uow, contract in its set) — never a bare foreign request id, never "other subject types skip". Regression seam tests/domain/imports/legacy_v1/test_contract_setup.py 222–285; fresh multi-contract composition; an EXTERNAL change after the initial job check but before a later plan consumes its base → atomic refusal, no partial events; existing stale-negatives and lock-order tests kept. **Docs:** dev-guide **1.51** (DG-KRN-APR-05 clause) ASSIGNED to P5. **Status:** P5's first candidate 687229c8 (generic mismatch-False shape) is WITHDRAWN as the landing shape (Codex 0226: baseline only); the refined implementation is gating on sprint/l12-p5; READY → Codex review → chain. "No evidence claims this race caused the parity failures" (the self-staleness did).

QUESTION (not a ruling; blocks only the ACTUAL mode-(a) dry run over the legacy fixture): ENGINE_SPEC S07-R-11 requires the OPENING_BALANCE_ESTABLISHED rows to carry the legacy values "as stored, converted by the shortest decimal string", but 04 §16.3 / `events/payloads.py` 513–529 type the money members of OpeningObligationV1 as MoneyIn whose MoneyStr regex (`money.py`:29) admits at most FOUR decimal places — the shipped legacy fixture (WLD-F-15, latest rows at cutover 2023-01-31) carries revenue_cum / remaining_allocation / netting_reclass_amount to 14 places and position_obligation to 15 (recorded 2026-09-20 21:31 PDT; reported by F-LMG while building the MIGRATION_IMPORT dry run; measured CPU-only with legacy_db + opening_balances.stage) The payload the dry run must append cannot represent S07-R-11's values. Resolution options, none the lane's to pick: (a) quantise the legacy values to 4 places on import — an ACCOUNTING / engine judgment (per obligation the error is ≤ 0.00005 < the 0.0001 tolerance, but contract sums over up to 7 rows could exceed it and the engine's exact revenue_cum would no longer equal legacy's); (b) widen MoneyStr or retype the OpeningObligationV1 money members to an exact decimal type (the kernel already represents exact values at 18 places — the posted + Q18 residue pattern of the trace) — a 04 §16.3 / payload / engine-input change owned OUTSIDE F-LMG (events / payloads and the s07 opening-balance stage); (c) carry the exact legacy value alongside the posted 4-place money member (value + residue, the trace's own pattern) — also a payload change. Interim (approved): the F-LMG applier REFUSES BY NAME when a legacy money member exceeds the payload's 4-place precision (naming S07-R-11 vs MoneyStr and the member) so the framework, tables, suppliers, job flow, unit tests and the NOT-RUN pg test land; the ACTUAL dry run over WLD-F-15 stays blocked until this is ruled. To Codex for the source read (is S07-R-11 the governing intent, and which owner holds the payload type); Ray's call only if (a) is chosen, since it changes migrated figures. #### 127 — REVISION 1 (2026-09-20 21:43 PDT; per Codex production-20260921-0442): an ENGINEERING admission-contract blocker with a scoped exact-value path — not an accounting question unless someone proposes quantisation; owner ASSIGNED Codex (committed main 0dea7b88 + design c02c4552): "ENGINE_SPEC S07-R-03 requires exact legacy full precision and S07-R-11 shortest stored decimal; OpeningObligationV1 has eight MoneyIn members, whose MoneyStr permits at most four fractional digits. The event/bundle engine path itself supports exact plain-decimal strings: engine_payload unwraps the amount string without rounding, and S07 opening.to_fraction consumes it exactly … This is an engineering admission-contract blocker, not evidence that the engine cannot calculate those values. Prefer a narrowly scoped exact opening/migration monetary representation preserving {amount,currency}, original numerical values, signed/currency checks and replay. DecimalStr already offers an 18-fractional/20-integer-digit plain-decimal primitive … Do not globally widen MoneyStr, silently round/pre-quantize, use model_construct/direct JSON/fake payload bypasses, or fabricate a successful capture." Requirements: reconcile 04 §16.3's Money designation and the API-S-Money minor-unit wording with the exact opening rule; follow the registered event version / upcast requirements; preserve old stored values / hashes / replay; exact plain-decimal normalisation where the shortest REAL text uses exponent notation, retaining the original row / text evidence; explicitly REFUSE out-of-bound values rather than round; booking line-price MoneyIn fields need field-specific admission analysis if legacy values exceed their bounds (fixing the eight opening members alone is not whole-import completion); witnesses carry original overprecision strings through the actual admitted event / bundle boundary and verify exact engine operands separately from posted outputs (negative / zero / currency; old payload behaviour; rollback / no capture on refusal). **Ruling:** the interim named over-precision refusal stays and the actual WLD dry run stays OPEN; the exact opening representation is a governed payload / schema design — OWNER: ENG-C6 / B4 (lane-eng-b4-05h; the events / rows / 04-tables lane) as the payload / schema owner, with F-LMG as the consuming lane and the S07 stage unchanged (already exact): docs-first proposal (04 **1.61** — §16.3 exact-decimal designation for the opening members and the API-S-Money wording reconciliation; the registered event version / upcast entry), then the code (a new registered event version or upcast of OpeningObligationV1 with DecimalStr-typed money members, amount + currency retained), tests per the witness list, Codex review. Quantisation is NOT approved and would be Ray's decision if ever proposed.

Putting the size ceiling at the API keeps one refusing control with a named 422 and an audit path; the proxy stays a coarse envelope that never masks the policy witness. 132. ENGINEERING ruling (2026-09-21 02:51 PDT; F-CLO CLO-10 in-flight request; supervisor, not Ray): the S14-R-17 held-only-currency gap is closed WITHOUT a schema change — `calculate` writes ONE run-level held detail file (FilePurpose.JOURNAL_EXPORT; every held line id, contract, hold, currency, amount) whenever held lines exist and records its file id + sha256 in the CALCULATE audit event's `after` beside the counts; the audit event is the retained link (no 04 column on T-SL-06 now; no zero journal entries; per-batch held lists unchanged). Coverage is by JOURNALISATION, not run range alone: a sealed line is covered iff a non-cancelled run's range holds its chain_seq AND its contract had no open journal_export hold at that run's created_at; lines held now and uncovered are named as held blockers; lines released after the run and never re-covered stay uncovered (release ≠ recovery); account totals compare journal vs covered activity only. Offsetting-uncovered and covered-and-netted (S14-R-18) witnesses are pure-rule tests. **Why:** a wholly held single-currency run must leave a retrievable, hashed record of what it withheld without inventing a journal entry or a column whose only reader is this file; the audit chain already binds run identity, actor and time, so the file id there is auditable evidence (D-97 audit rules) and a later 04 column can be added docs-first if a second reader appears. Coverage by journalisation follows Codex 0908 §5 / 0920 §2–§3: a run that skipped a held line cannot be credited with covering it. **How to apply:** code + tests in the CLO-10 slice (completeness.py / validation.py / gates.py as written; the held-file writer in summarise.calculate; witness: wholly held EUR-only run → run row, zero batches, held file retains the ids, completeness FAILED naming them; after release still FAILED and a fresh run does not cover them); DB-bound cases written NOT RUN on the lane; the twelve batch-#4 close cases stay RED until the next integrated batch measures them. If a 04 column is later preferred, it is docs-first under a number assigned on request.

ENGINEERING rulings (2026-09-21 02:55 PDT; F-LMG workstream 2 design note, record §26 at 2ea6b9dd, Q1 / Q2; supervisor, not Ray): **Q1 → the lane's proposal.** For a "Will be created" entity the confirmed mapping does not carry: functional currency = the tenant reporting currency (the LMG-2 acceptance wording); calendar_id = the tenant's ONLY fiscal calendar when exactly one exists, else a confirmed value; time zone = a confirmed value. `EntityMappingIn` gains optional `calendar_id` / `time_zone`; `OpeningBalancesImportIn` gains `entity_defaults {calendar_id?, time_zone?}` (a per-entity value wins over the batch default); `/import` refuses by name (422, the field named, one finding per unresolvable entity) when a to-be-created entity has no resolvable calendar or time zone — no invented values; the resolved values are part of the confirmation captured at `/import` (D-98 128 set-once) and are listed in the `migration_batch.prerequisites` event. The alternative (one batch parameter `migration.entity_time_zone` + the single-calendar rule) is REJECTED: legacy books span time zones and a batch-level value cannot express a per-entity fact. **Q2 → `create_missing_products`**, an import parameter default TRUE mirroring LM-CL-09 / LM-SSP-02: name = code; `default_pob_template_id` = the row's parity template (LEGACY-DISTINCT / LEGACY-NONDISTINCT per the POL-211 choice / LEGACY-MATERIAL-RIGHT / LEGACY-VC); distinctness from the row; no SSP entries; a SKU mapped to two templates is refused by name BEFORE any write; created products are listed in the prerequisites event; with the flag false a missing product is a named S07-R-03 refusal, never a silent skip. "Refuse and require the SKU import first" is REJECTED for mode (a): no SKU import exists there, and the confirmed mapping already carries code, template and distinctness. **Why:** the migration import already has one confirmation step whose captured content is set-once (D-98 128); every value a created entity or product needs must either be derivable from the confirmed mapping or be confirmed there by name — nothing may be invented downstream, and nothing may require a second, unrelated confirmation of data the operator has already confirmed. Consistency with the existing "create confirmed missing" mechanics (LM-CL-09 / LM-SSP-02) over a new pattern; `_check_prerequisites` stays the fail-closed reader (a differently-templated product or a disabled reporting currency is still refused, never re-templated / enabled). **How to apply:** docs first — 04 **1.64** (§17.2 LM-CL-09 row: calendar / time-zone / currency inputs; LM-CL-03 `create_missing_products`), BUILD_SPEC header **1.11** (the 13 LMG-2 paths hunk), BUILD_SPEC.md regenerated (`--check` clean); SCREENS_B §10.3 (entity-mapping calendar / time-zone columns) is the frontend owner's document — F-LMG reports the needed change to the supervisor for routing and does not edit it; then `domain/migration/prerequisites.py` plan / apply called from `jobs.import_batch` phase IMPORT after the S07-R-03 findings and BEFORE `capture.dry_run`, in the OUTER job transaction (durable with IMPORTED, or nothing) under the job's principal (masterdata.maintain), idempotent by code, never updating an existing row; the reference writers audit AUD-CMD and one `migration_batch.prerequisites` event lists what the batch created. Tests as designed: tests/domain/migration/test_prerequisites.py (plan over WLD-F-15; conflicting-SKU refusal; idempotence), the import-job unit (apply before dry_run in the outer unit; skipped when the flags are false), the pg capture test over WLD-F-15 with NO provisioned entities / products → IMPORTED (NOT RUN), and the LMG-2 case test_entity_mapping_creates_missing_entities made real. Ruling: emit progress_ratio under a guard / no-FIXED at every period and at "-" as the rule-produced value 0 via a NEW formula `rec.progress.unmeasured.v1` (arity 0; params as_of, reason ∈ {V4:<status>, V5, S09-R-13:LEASE, no-fixed-component}) and link it unconditionally — a rule-valued 0 like the guarded revenue_cum node, NOT a third absence reason (the absence vocabulary is reserved for states no rule values). Docs first: ENGINE_SPEC_B §9.5 progress_ratio row, ENGINE_SPEC §0.10 stage-09 formula row + narrative. Constraints (Codex 1249 §1): truthful guarded-progress provenance preserving the actual guarded zero / status / date; do NOT activate the fixture to dodge the gap, map to a nonexistent / wrong-measure node, or echo an unexplained zero; SEPARATE committed witnesses — the four-obligation unmodified baseline and the five-obligation modified world (status / guard, exact subject population, producing node, replay, final linkage) — retaining an unguarded positive path; all existing allocation / zero-total / created-VC / NULL-SSP controls kept. Emitter change in Codex 1249 §2's shape: keep posted P_minor = Σ(C_after − C_before) unchanged; emit uniquely event / side-qualified exact ENDPOINT nodes (Fraction major-unit E_before / E_after at the event-specific ENG-06 positions) and an independently replayable exact-difference / sum formula over them (a precomputed-delta SourceRef or a bare residue is insufficient); residue = A_major − P_minor / 10^mu; check BOTH endpoints of EVERY admitted event (the final version's adjusted flag proves nothing about earlier endpoints); replay cross-checks the linked posted node's value + residue against the replayed exact companion under governed encoding; older traces lacking exact provenance are explicitly unavailable; value stays the posted sum; posted chain, quantities, event membership, journal amounts and 1e-4 unchanged. Limit: an `adjusted` endpoint (manual adjustment, hold freeze, S02-R-04 / STEP1 netting) is defined in posted units by its rule — no exact counterpart exists and none may be manufactured; such endpoints get separately justified exact provenance or an EXPLICIT unavailable / refusal with the basis named (`exact_basis = posted-only (adjusted target)`), never segment-exact substitution or silent posted-as-exact; guarded positions exact 0 = value 0. Witness: the activated Contract 2 step 09 LEGACY_PARITY world, ASC606, v = 2023-05-31 — POB #3 posted activity 56.64 vs exact 26000/459, residue 56/11475 ≈ 0.00488 (> 1e-4); POB #1 residue 37/40950. Spec: one CV-64 sentence ("the exact activity of a version is the sum over the same new events of the exact segment-target differences at the same positions; an adjusted target carries no exact and the residue is 0 with its basis named"). Aside adopted as a docs-first correction: ENGINE_SPEC_B §9.5 names revenue_cum's formula `sched.cumulative_posted.v1` while the registry / code emit `rec.revenue_cum.v1` — the document is corrected to the registered id. **Why:** a NOT NULL column published with a 0 and no producer violates the linkage contract for a state a RULE does value (the guard defines progress as unmeasured), so the fix is a rule-produced node, not an absence; the exact activity operand is already computed by the evaluator and merely unbound, so binding it costs no new state and removes the provenance gap that made a node-plus-residue rendering impossible for Current Rev Rec (Codex 1155 §1); adjusted targets have no exact by definition and must say so rather than invent one. **How to apply (ENG-T1F, after READY-4's landing; numbers assigned): ENGINE_SPEC 1.30 = T1F-89-1 (the CV-64 sentence); ENGINE_SPEC 1.31 = T1F-LP-PR-1 (§0.10 formula row + narrative); ENGINE_SPEC_B 1.36 = the §9.5 progress_ratio row + the §9.5 revenue_cum formula-id correction.** Docs first, then code + the two witnesses (POB #3 residue; the golden step 07 check_book with no exceptions), gates, record, READY. Its premise (trace.py `exact_companion_failures` docstring: "both are 18-place encodings of numbers that differ by the posted value, so the encodings translate") is false — signed HALF_UP18 does not commute with translation when A and A − P sit on opposite-signed half-way ties (Codex's ordinary FIXED example, verified by Codex with stdlib Fraction: P = 1.01, A = 1.0049999999999999995, Q18(A) = 1.005, Q18(A − P) = −0.005000000000000001, P + Q18(A − P) = 1.004999999999999999 ≠ Q18(A)), so `_assert_exact_companions` raises ENGINE_INVARIANT_VIOLATED / CV-64 on an admitted ordinary delivery — a production rejection of valid input. **Rule:** each stored field is validated against ITS OWN encoding of the replayed exact — `companion.value == Q18(A_replayed)` AND `rounding_residue == Q18(A_replayed − P)` — where A_replayed is re-derived by `reevaluate` through the companion's own formula chain (`rec.exact_activity.v1` over the `rec.exact_difference.v1` deltas over the bound endpoint operands), never taken from a raw params copy; the two checks are independent and neither is derived from the other. **Forbidden repairs:** any tolerance (the 1e-4 financial tolerance is not extended to this check), a currency-rounding change, omitting the check, or marking the case exact-unavailable (it is a supported ordinary input). Posted P, quantities, event membership, journal amounts, all monetary oracles, CV-35 and CV-51 unchanged; the existing companion / residue mutation refusals retained. **Docs first, IN PLACE (ENGINE_SPEC 1.30 is unlanded — same number, no new revision):** the CV-64 sentence "trace validation checks `value + rounding_residue` against the replayed companion under the CV-51 encoding" and the 1.30 revision-row parenthetical become the per-field encoding-aware rule; the docstring follows the document. **Witnesses:** the signed-tie case in Codex's units authored at the production compute seam (compute → `assert_identities` → `_assert_exact_companions` admits it) AND at the explanation-verification seam (explain service with `replayed`), plus one negative per field (a mutated companion; a mutated residue) still refused by name. **Precondition stated before coding:** the lane's design note names how A_replayed is exact from the stored chain (which raw operands CV-51 binds at the endpoints and the delta) — if the exact A cannot be recovered from the stored chain, the note says so and the rule is re-ruled; no silent oracle choice.
